# Fanxiu Reverse Context

> Last Updated: 2026-05-26
> Audience: future Codex/agent sessions working on 凡人修仙传手游 static/resource/protocol analysis in this CodeYun workspace.

This document is the handoff map for the current Fanxiu reverse-analysis work. Keep it high-density and practical: paths, tools, proven chains, current boundaries, and where to update things next.

## Scope

Current work is focused on static analysis, resource indexing, local catalog/wiki generation, and protocol/authority-boundary understanding. Do not treat this document as an instruction to modify APKs, inject code, bypass auth, or automate unfair gameplay.

When continuing this line of work:

- Put generated analysis outputs under the existing export root.
- Prefer adding reusable backend logic in CodeYun over one-off scripts.
- Keep reports reproducible and summarize major new findings in the runtime notes file.
- Do not copy account tokens, live credentials, or private identifiers into docs.

Cpp2IL found metadata version `24.5`, ELF AArch64, CodeRegistration `0x2F1B810`, MetadataRegistration `0x2F1C210`, and mapped 66734 method pointers.

Current PATH check on `2026-05-25`: `cpp2il`, `jadx`, `apktool`, `java`, and `dotnet` are not globally available, but the portable Java/JADX and Cpp2IL paths above are verified. Use absolute tool paths or a temporary per-command `PATH`/`JAVA_HOME`; do not assume global installs. If a new CLI/portable tool is needed, an agent may download, checksum/verify when practical, unpack under `D:\tools\...`, use it with absolute paths, and record the exact path here without user intervention. Stop for user input before GUI-only installers, license/activation prompts, kernel/network drivers, admin-risky system changes, account credentials, or anything that would move beyond static/resource/protocol observation. Frontend smoke testing has a repo-external Playwright package at `D:\tools\codeyun-playwright\node_modules\playwright`; it is not a codeyun dependency, and current browser checks use the installed Chrome executable path because Playwright's bundled browser is not installed.

## CodeYun Implementation Map

Backend routes are mounted through `backend/api/fanxiu_resources.py`. Most reverse-analysis endpoints are `POST /api/fanxiu/resources/...` wrappers around backend core builders.

Important backend modules:

| File | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_resources.py` | Resource roots, Unity bundle inspection/export, Wwise bank probing. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_apk_static.py` | APK/Dex/Manifest/network/login/static reports. This is the main APK-side static-analysis module. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_il2cpp_metadata.py` | Metadata-only IL2CPP index/probes before method bodies are recovered. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_download_bridge.py` | Lua download bridge and IL2CPP download inventory reports. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_hot_update.py` | Hot-update Lua/config probes for BLLD, BlueStarSea, Faze, authority boundaries. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_game_luaconfig.py` | Parsed game config/catalog work, especially Gongfa/Lingjie feature catalogs. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_gongfa_catalog.py` | Structured Gongfa/wiki card catalog and rich text sections. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_doupotd_catalog.py` | Structured `doupotd` partner/card catalog plus skill/buff/result/reward-config probes from DoupoTowerDefense, DoupoCardCompose, and visible doupotd Lua. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_digitdoor_catalog.py` | Structured `digitdoor` config catalog: character cards, skill show/logic rows, door effects, level/door refresh summaries, door pool/customizedType semantics, door gain-buff boundary reports, separated custom/rogue `SkillEnhance` groups, plus `SkillEnhanceEffect`, `BuffEffect`, Buff class formula, monster refresh, RewardResult, reward marker, and item-corner UI reports. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_item_catalog.py` | Item card/search catalog. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_lua_logic_index.py` | Lua logic/runtime indexing reports. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_lua_packet_index.py` | Lua protocol packet indexing. |
| `backend/core/fanxiu_protocol_semantics.py` | Shared loader for feature protocol semantics TSV/edge TSV data; backs the CodeYun protocol semantics page. |

Frontend surfaces:

| File/Route | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `frontend/src/standard/fanxiu/wiki/page.vue` | Main Fanxiu wiki/catalog UI. Current priority is readable item/Gongfa/doupotd/digitdoor text, rich-text style rendering, grouped duplicate entries, game-like detail panels, doupotd `斗破奖励`, and digitdoor character/skill/door-effect/level/monster-wave browsing. |
| `frontend/src/standard/fanxiu/packet-capture/page.vue` | Local packet-capture helper UI. |
| `frontend/src/standard/fanxiu/protocol-semantics/page.vue` (`/fanxiu/protocol-semantics`) | Protocol semantics inspector for BlueStarSea/BLLD/Faze/Gongfa packet rows and semantic edges. Supports URL initialization with `?feature=gongfa&q=GongFaUpgrade`. |
| `frontend/src/standard/fanxiu/index.ts` | Fanxiu page route registration. Remember side-menu visibility is controlled separately by `frontend/src/layout/MainLayout.vue` and `frontend/src/features/access/permissionRegistry.json`. |

Main regression test:

```powershell
uv run pytest backend/tests/test_fanxiu_resources.py -q
```

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd Select pair: `uv run pytest backend/tests/test_fanxiu_resources.py -q` is `153 passed`. Current Fanxiu resource route count remains `185`. The new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupotdselect_sm_doupotdselect_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DoupoTDSelect(93648)` has `type/grade`, both assigned in `CM_DoupoTDSelectFun(type, grade)`. `SM_DoupoTDSelect(93649)` has `selectVOS/canRandomValue/type/grade`; `selectVOS` maps through `VO_URL` to `DoupoTDSelectVO(93647)` with `index/type/id`. Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd Effect pair: `uv run pytest backend/tests/test_fanxiu_resources.py -q` is `153 passed`. Current Fanxiu resource route count remains `185`. The new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupotdeffect_sm_doupotdeffect_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DoupoTDEffect(93651)` has `index`, assigned from the chosen upgrade candidate index in `DoupoTDUpgradeSkillView:ChooseSkill`. `SM_DoupoTDEffect(93652)` has `effectVO`, which resolves to `DoupoTDEffectVO(93650)` with `index/type/id`. On success, `SM_DoupoTDEffectFun` calls `Model:UpdateRoleSkillAttrList(msg.effectVO)`, raises `DoupoTDUpgradeSkill` and `RefreshSceneRoleArea`. In `DoupoTDModel.UpdateRoleSkillAttrList`, `type==2` updates `DoupoTowerDefense_SkillEnhance` per-character skill counters and selected list, while `type==3` consumes `DoupoTowerDefense_TurnSkillEnhance.rogueId`; `type==1` is treated by the handler as a newly obtained role id list.

Visible callers are closed: `DoupoTDMgr.UpdateActivationActivityVO` and `CheckActivityActivation` call `CM_DoupoTDInfoFun()` when the doupotd activity/PVE state is open or changes.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardPanel pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardpanel_sm_doupocardpanel_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardPanel(95612)` has no request fields. `SM_DouPoCardPanel(95613)` returns `pageValue/cardVOList/taskRewardList/allCardNum/currRewardId`. `SM_DouPoCardPanelFun` checks `code`, stores the whole response as `DoupoTDModel.V_LastestDrawInfo`, raises `DouPoDrawCard_Draw_Reward` red dot, and broadcasts `DoupoTDType.EventType.DouPoPartnerCardPanel,msg`. UI consumers confirm the schema meaning: `DoupoTDCompositingPanel` reads `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum` for card composition pages, while `DoupoTDDrawPanel` reads `currRewardId` to update the current draw-progress reward. Generated packet writing treats `taskRewardList` as an int list, while `cardVOList` elements resolve through `DouPoCardVO(95603).resourceId`.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardDraw pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocarddraw_sm_doupocarddraw_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardDraw(95601)` sends `id/times`, both assigned by `DoupoTDDrawPanel.OnClickDrawBtn` from the selected `DoupoCardCompose_DrawCard` row and the available draw-item count capped at `10`. `SM_DouPoCardDraw(95602)` returns `douPoCardVOS/rewardResults/allCardNum/currRewardId`. The response handler does not write a model cache; it raises `DouPoDrawCard_Draw_Reward` and broadcasts `DoupoTDType.EventType.DouPoPartnerCardDrawEvent,msg`. `DoupoTDDrawPanel.OnDrawEvent` consumes that event: it refreshes draw-item state, plays the draw animation, converts each `DouPoCardVO.resourceId` into a display reward by `GetPartnerInfoVoByResourceId(resourceId).showItem`, appends `msg.rewardResults`, and opens the reward popup after the animation delay.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardTaskReward pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardtaskreward_sm_doupocardtaskreward_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardTaskReward(95614)` sends `id`, taken from `DoupoTDDrawPanel.currentTaskRewardId` when the player clicks the big progress reward area and `V_IsEnough` is true. `SM_DouPoCardTaskReward(95615)` returns `id/rewardResults/alreadyNum/currRewardId`; the handler consumes `rewardResults` and `currRewardId`, opens the reward popup, calls `DoupoTDModel:SetCurrentTaskReward(currRewardId)`, raises the draw-reward red dot, and broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardTaskReward,msg`. `SetCurrentTaskReward` ensures `V_LastestDrawInfo` exists as an `SM_DouPoCardPanel` object and writes `V_LastestDrawInfo.currRewardId=currentRewardId`; `DoupoTDDrawPanel.OnClaimTaskRewardEvent` then updates its local `currentTaskRewardId` and refreshes the progress view.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardCompose pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardcompose_sm_doupocardcompose_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardCompose(95608)` sends `pageValue/one/two`. The visible caller is `DoupoTDCompositingPanel`: after a drag/drop overlap, it validates both cards have the same `charId`, same `quality`, same `resourceID`, different indexes, and not max star, then sends `CM_DouPoCardComposeFun(self.currentIndex,index2,index1)`. `SM_DouPoCardCompose(95609)` returns `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`; handler only broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardCompositing,msg`. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnSingleCompositingEvent` consumes the response by setting `currentIndex`, refreshing `cardVOList/allCardNum`, and playing normal or special effect based on the source card quality versus max quality.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardComposeAll pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardcomposeall_sm_doupocardcomposeall_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardComposeAll(95606)` has no request fields. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnBatchBtnClick` sends it only when the panel is not tweening and `DoupoTDModel:GetCanCompositingCardReDot()` says batch composition is available. `SM_DouPoCardComposeAll(95607)` returns `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`; handler only broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardCompositingAll,msg`. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnCompositingAllEvent` consumes it by setting `currentIndex`, calling `ShowCompositingAllEffect`, moving cards to center, playing two effects, then refreshing `cardVOList/allCardNum` and clearing `isTweenning`.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardMerge pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardmerge_sm_doupocardmerge_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardMerge(95604)` has no request fields and is sent by `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnCollatingBtnClick`. `SM_DouPoCardMerge(95605)` returns `cardVOList/pageValue`; handler only broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardPageSort,msg`. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnCardMergeEvent` consumes the response by setting `currentIndex`, forcing all items visible, and refreshing `cardVOList`. Unlike `Panel/Compose/ComposeAll`, this response does not include `allCardNum`.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardJumpPage pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardjumppage_sm_doupocardjumppage_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardJumpPage(95616)` sends `jumpPageValue`, assigned from the requested page index in `DoupoTDCompositingPanel` previous/next callbacks. `SM_DouPoCardJumpPage(95617)` returns `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`; handler checks `code` and broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardPageChange,msg`. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnCommonRefreshEvent` listens to the page-change event and refreshes the same card-list surface as the panel snapshot: it sets `currentIndex=msg.pageValue or 1`, forces item visibility, then calls `UpdateScrollView(msg.cardVOList,msg.allCardNum)`.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardRedCan pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardredcan_sm_doupocardredcan_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardRedCan(95620)` is registered and has no fields, but no visible Lua send function/source caller was found in the current export. `SM_DouPoCardRedCan(95621)` returns `can:Bool`; handler calls `DoupoTDModel:SetCanCompositingCardReDot(msg)` and raises `RedDotID.DouPoDrawCard_Compositing`. `SetCanCompositingCardReDot` writes `V_CanCompositingCardReDot=msg.can`, and `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnBatchBtnClick` reads it through `GetCanCompositingCardReDot()` to decide whether batch composition can run. Treat this as a server-synced composition-availability flag with a request-side source gap, not as a direct user action.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd DouPoCardTopQ pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupocardtopq_sm_doupocardtopq_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DouPoCardTopQ(95618)` has no fields and is visibly requested from card-mode partner/up-battle entry points: `DoupoTDPartnerPanel`, `DoupoTDUpBattleView`, and `DoupoTDMgr.EnterScene`. `SM_DouPoCardTopQ(95619)` returns `id2q/id2Star`; handler calls `DoupoTDModel:DouPoCardTopQUpdateFun(msg)`, which writes `DoupoTDData._cardMaxQualityDic[id]=quality` and `_cardMaxStarDic[id]=star`, then raises `DouPoPartnerCardMaxQualityEvent`. Consumers read `GetCardCurMaxQuality(id)` and `GetCardCurMaxStar(id)` to render card-mode partner backgrounds and star text in `DoupoTDPartnerItem`, `DoupoTDSceneUpBattleRoleItem`, `DoupoTDPartnerHeadUI`, and related club/immortal doupo views. Defaults are quality `1` and star `0` when no entry exists.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd Upgrade pair: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupotdupgrade_sm_doupotdupgrade_pair_flow_report.md`; generated checks all pass. `CM_DoupoTDUpgrade(93605)` sends `addExp`, assigned by `DoupoTDEntityMgr.ExpChange` when accumulated local exp crosses a level threshold, and also resent after reconnect if `sendServerExp > 0`. Before server acknowledgement, the client updates local `PlayerGrade/PlayerExp` for immediate scene UI feedback and stores pending exp in `sendServerExp`. `SM_DoupoTDUpgrade(93606)` returns `exp/oldGrade/grade`; handler subtracts confirmed exp from `sendServerExp` and calls `DoupoTDMgr:DelayShowUpgradeSkillView(oldGrade,grade)`. That delayed path waits until no dial/info/skill-tip overlay is active, pauses the scene, then opens `DoupoTDUpgradeSkillView` so the next `Select -> Effect` skill-choice chain can run.

Latest addendum after drilling the doupotd SkipLevel single-flow path: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_cm_doupotdskiplevel_single_flow_report.md`. `CM_DoupoTDSkipLevel(93630)` has no fields and no dedicated visible `SM_DoupoTDSkipLevel`. `DoupoTDInfoPanel.OnEnterClick` enables skip only when `showLevelCfg.allowSkipLevel ~= 999` and the player's total level reaches that threshold; confirmation sends `CM_DoupoTDSkipLevelFun()`, while cancel enters the scene normally. The authoritative outcome is folded into `SM_DoupoTDGamePlayer.isSkipLevel`: skip results bypass the normal result view, may show rewards and newly unlocked characters, reset `isSkipLevel`, and raise `DoupoTDInfoUpdate(msg.isSkipLevel)` so the info panel refreshes.

Latest addendum after reviewing doupotd server-only handlers: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_server_only_handlers_report.md`. `SM_DoupoTDActivityEnd(93638)` has no packet-specific fields and simply calls `DoupoTDMgr:ReqFinishGame()` after a successful base result check. `SM_DoupoTDGMBattle(93669)` carries `battleVO:ImmortalBattleVO`, caches it through `ImmortalDoupoMgr.Model.ImmortalData:UpdateFinishVo`, then requests replay on scene `87104`. `SM_DoupoTDGMClubBattle(93674)` carries `clubPkDoupoRecordVO:ClubPkDoupoRecordVO`, caches it through `ClubPkDoupoMgr.Model.ClubPkDoupoData:UpdateFinishVo`, then requests replay on scene `87105`. These are server-only push/debug/replay entry points, not normal user-action pairs.

Latest addendum after generating the doupotd packet coverage inventory: the new report is `apk_static_index/lua_lscript_module_doupotd_packet_coverage_inventory_report.md`. Visible `DoupoTDNetLogic.lua` registers `58` packets: `28` client-to-server and `30` server-to-client. Coverage is complete at the static registration level: `54` packets are covered by pair reports, `1` by the `SkipLevel` single-flow report, and `3` by the server-only handlers report; unclassified packet registrations are `0`. Remaining known doupotd gaps are source-placement/schema-version issues already documented separately, not missing packet registrations.

## Output Directory Map

Under `frxx_analysis_exports`:

| Directory | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `apk_static_index` | APK, manifest, network, login, IL2CPP, Cpp2IL, hot-update report markdown/TSV/JSON outputs. |
| `parsed_configs` | Extracted Lua/config tables and derived catalogs. |
| `parsed_configs/audio_catalog` | Static Wwise `.bnk`/`.wem` bank, chunk, and embedded WEM entry metadata catalog. |
| `parsed_configs/gongfa_catalog` | Gongfa object catalog, runtime notes, rich-text section outputs. |
| `parsed_configs/item_catalog` | Item text/card catalog. |
| `parsed_configs/lingjie_feature_catalog` | Lingjie/Gongfa feature relationship and runtime reports. |
| `parsed_configs/lua_packet_index` | Lua packet/protocol catalog: packet ids, read/write field order, MessagePool registrations, handler mapping. |
| `parsed_configs/asset_catalog` | Static Unity model/effect/UI/scene/animation asset inventory and metadata catalog. |
| `parsed_configs/visual_catalog` | Static Unity atlas/APK icon, logo, title-label, portrait, skill, fashion, and SDK image catalog with exported PNG gallery. |
| `icons` | Exported/curated sprite/icon assets used by the wiki UI. |
| `previews`, `by_source`, `indexes` | Resource previews and source indexes from earlier Unity/resource exploration. |

Private/runtime state probe artifacts:

| Artifact | Path / Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Private state snapshot | `private_state_probe/` mirrors `/data/data/com.frxxcrjpwssc3.ggws` via read-only ADB/root observation. Treat raw files as local-only sensitive material. |
| Redacted private state summaries | `private_state_probe/analysis/*.redacted.json` plus `analysis/README.md`; use these for agent-facing context instead of raw SharedPreferences/cache values. |
| Live value probe | `private_state_probe_live_115709/analysis/probe_115709_findings.md`; target value `115709` was not found in private files, ordinary writable memory, or the short TCP sample using common encodings. |
| Short TCP live sample | `private_state_probe_live_115709/analysis/tcp_probe_115709.summary.json`; only `857` bytes / estimated `10` packets, no useful FightResult-family evidence. |

Private-state conclusion: local private files mostly expose SDK, push, analytics, Bugly, downloader, Unity playerprefs, and Volley cache state. They do not expose complete backpack/Gongfa/task progress. If ownership or live numeric state is needed later, prefer a carefully scoped runtime packet/log/UI observation plan with redaction, not raw private-state dumping.

## Current Mental Model

High-level architecture discovered so far:

1. TapTap/MuMu/Android side launches a Unity IL2CPP game wrapped by Java SDK/channel code.
2. Initial config and resources are HTTP/HTTPS downloads from `akbing.com` hosts.
3. The actual game business protocol is not mostly browser-like GET/POST. After server selection, it uses binary socket packets through Lua/Unity bridge code.
4. Hot-update Lua/resources carry most gameplay-specific logic and table data. APK metadata mostly exposes bridge types and method names.
5. IL2CPP method bodies require pairing `global-metadata.dat` with `libil2cpp.so`; metadata alone is not enough.
6. Cpp2IL is now usable locally and can recover enough ISIL to follow selected method bodies.
7. The server-list HTTP response-to-socket target transition is now closed on the Lua side.
8. The first socket login packet send path is now closed on the Lua side through `SocketBridge.F_Send`.
9. The `SM_Login(20002)` receive-side login initialization path is now closed on the Lua side through `LoginNetLogic.SM_LoginData -> EnterGameInfo.StartEnter_4/5 -> CM_LoginFinish`.
10. The post-login `LoginFinishGetServerData()` fanout is now mapped into first sync calls and packet pairs, with manager-only entries separated from closed socket requests.
11. The first-level nested VO schema inside `SM_Login(20002)` is now indexed: `RoleVO`, `MapInfoVO`, `FunctionOpenVo`, `CrossVO`, and `SimpleClubVO`.
12. Typed second-level login snapshot beans are now indexed: `HangPointVO`, `Grid3DVO`, and `SM_SkillVO`.
13. The IL2CPP send bridge after `SocketBridge.F_Send` is now mapped through `SocketManager.F_Send`, `ByteSocket.F_Send`, `LusuoStreamQuick`, and `Socket.BeginSend`.
14. Self-created Gongfa chat-share href resolution is closed at the generic TextEx/Lua callback and HyperLinkMgr consumer levels, but the chat-label static click entry is not closed. 42. Socket primitive decoding is now mapped to typed pools. True run outputs `9` primitive rules, `5` flow rows, and `14/14` evidence rows. Generated Lua packet classes do not directly read raw TCP bytes: `BaseMessage.readInt/readByte/readShort/readBool` consume `SocketPoolData.GetCurIntVal`, `readLong` consumes two `GetCurUIntVal` values and constructs `LusuoLong.new(low, high)`, and float/double/string use their own typed pools. C#/IL2CPP `PoolMessageManage` reads the raw stream through `LusuoStreamQuick`, fills `CCSocketPoolData` lists, then `CsCallLuaMgr.ReceiveSocketMessage` passes typed lists into Lua `SocketManager.ReceiveSocketMessage`. Practical implication: runtime observation should prefer the typed pool bridge first; a raw TCP decoder still needs the lower `LusuoStreamQuick/PoolMessageManage` layer.

44. A raw socket decoder outline is now mapped below the typed-pool layer. True run outputs `6` frame rows, `7` primitive rows, and `12/12` evidence rows. Static Cpp2IL/ISIL evidence supports: send path writes `WriteNoCompress(totalLength) -> headerStream(sn, proId) -> bodyStream -> Socket.BeginSend`; receive path reads a 4-byte outer header with `ByteUtil.ReadInt(..., IsLittleEndian=false)`, builds `LusuoStreamQuick(..., isMsgCompress)`, then performs three leading `ReadInt` calls before `PoolMessageManage.IsLuaMessage/PoolMessageManage.read`. `ReadInt` branches to `ByteUtil.ReadIntCompress` unless uncompressed/forced; `ReadBigString` and `ReadBigStringByte` are length-prefixed. Exact compressed-int byte reconstruction should still be calibrated with a small packet fixture because Cpp2IL leaves part of `ByteUtil.ReadIntCompress` as invalid ARM instructions.
45. The compressed int path now has an executable candidate codec. True run outputs `5` codec rules, `16` roundtrip samples, and `5/5` evidence rows. The candidate is standard signed int32 zigzag plus 7-bit varint chunks: `u=((value<<1)^(value>>31))&0xffffffff`, continuation bytes use `0x80`, and decode is `(u>>1) ^ -(u&1)`. This matches `WriteIntCompress`, `revertSign32`, and `ReadInt` branch evidence, and all generated samples roundtrip. It is still labeled candidate, not final live-wire proof, until checked against a captured/fixture packet byte sequence.
46. An existing decoded TCP fixture now calibrates the compressed-int body-length model. Calibration rule: `frame_len == len(encode_int_compress(sn)) + len(encode_int_compress(pro_id)) + payload_len`. This confirms the candidate codec against real local capture metadata without exporting parsed payload values. The fixture contains no `SM_FightResult` family frames, so it calibrates socket framing/codec only, not combat result semantics.

70. The Java SDK shell is now resolved one layer deeper. The main APK carries `assets/sq_plugin_3.7.9.6.1_2025_11_28_10_43_37.apk`, loads it through `PluginManager.copyAssetPlugin(..., "plugin")` and `ApkClassLoader`, then `PluginSQwanCore` reflects into `com.sqwan.msdk.SQwanCoreImpl`. True run over the SQ plugin JADX output scans `9091` plugin Java files, records `41` structural evidence rows, and confirms `SQwanCoreImpl -> BaseSQwanCore.login/doLogin -> _SQwan/Platform.loginSQ -> com.sy37sdk.core.SQwan.login -> Platform.sqLoginSuccess -> Bundle(token,gid,pid) -> SQResultListener.onSuccess`. This remains static/template evidence only; no live token values are collected.
71. The 37 SDK account layer is now mapped statically. True run over the same SQ plugin JADX source records `106` account/login evidence rows and confirms `SQwan.login -> SQwanManager.login -> IAccountMod.login -> AccountModImpl.login -> UIVersionManager/FastVerifyController -> AccountLoginManager/AccountLogic`. The normal account-password path is a signed HTTP POST to `UrlConstant.LOGIN`, with field names `uname/upwd/D-Token/trans_info` and common SDK params. Password text is AES-encrypted with an appKey-derived 16-byte key before request. Phone password/code/ticket, one-key/flash login, and WeChat login are separate SDK HTTP routes. `handleLoginSuccess` parses `uid/uname/token`, adds `pid/gid`, and calls the listener map that becomes the SQwan Bundle. This is endpoint/field-name evidence only, not a license to read local account cache or capture live credentials.
72. The sy37 request envelope is now mapped statically. True run over `9091` SQ plugin Java files records `221` request-signing evidence rows and confirms `SqRequest.signV3()` only tags the request; the shared OkHttp chain then runs `RequestIdOkInterceptor -> SignInterceptor -> GateWayEncryptInterceptor`. `CommonParamsV2` has all `13/13` expected public fields and is used by account-password login; `CommonParamsV3` has all `17/17` expected public/device fields and is used by phone/ticket/flash/WeChat login paths. `SignV3Interceptor` signs sorted params, excludes existing `sign`, appends the appKey-derived tail, and emits lowercase MD5; secure endpoints may then pass through gateway query/body encryption. This report records shape and boundaries only: it does not output appKey/runtime gateway keys, collect device/account identifiers, or generate a replay client.
73. The sy37 server-info URL update layer is now mapped statically. True run over `9091` SQ plugin Java files records `273` URL-update evidence rows and `123` `@UrlUpdate` annotation rows. `BaseSQwanCore.init` calls `UpdateUrlManager.reqUrlUpdateManager`, which requests `UrlConstant.UPDATE_URL` with `signV3 + CommonParamsV1`; response `api_infos[]` is parsed as `api_key/api_info`, ordinary entries populate `apiMap/apiUrls`, while `x_secure_key` is separated for `GateWayManager.updateKeySet`. `UrlUpdateUtils` uses `UrlMod` and runtime-retained `@UrlUpdate(value,xValue)` annotations to reflect dynamic URLs back into static fields and `sq_url_update` preferences. Dynamic secure URLs also update the gateway whitelist and HTTPDNS protect-host list. Treat hardcoded `UrlConstant` values as defaults/bootstrap evidence, not guaranteed final runtime URLs.

The class rollup shows `com.sy37sdk.account.UrlConstant` owns `52` rows with `34` secure-like defaults, `com.sy37sdk.core.INewUrl` owns `37`, `IMUrl` owns `13`, and order/liveshow/plugin/share/common classes fill the rest. 91. `CM_DoupoTDGamePlayer/SM_DoupoTDGamePlayer` is now drilled as the first focused doupotd packet pair. The client sends `currWave/wavePercent` plus local battle summary from `DoupoTDEntityMgr:GetTotalKillSmallMonsterNum()` and `GetTotalBossDamageList()`; the server returns `finishWave/rewardResults/passLevelVOS/levelId/gameType/isSkipLevel/wavePercent`. The response handler exits the TD session, opens the result view or reward/new-character popups, writes finish state through `DoupoTDMgr/DoupoTDData`, and raises `DoupoTDInfoUpdate`. This pair is an end-of-run/progress-summary boundary: the client reports local progress snapshots, while rewards/pass-level state are consumed from the server response.
92. `CM_DoupoTDRefreshWave/SM_DoupoTDRefreshWave` is now drilled as the second focused doupotd packet pair. The client sends requested `wave`, fixed `addExp=LusuoLong.FromNumber(0)`, current kill count, and boss HP snapshot; the server returns `refreshWave/currExp`. The response handler writes `refreshWave` through `DoupoTDData:SetWave`, which sets `V_CurWave`, then raises `DoupoTDType.EventType.RefreshWave` with the full message. This pair is a wave-refresh state correction boundary: the client proposes local progress and boss snapshot, while the authoritative current wave and experience are consumed from the server response.
93. `CM_DoupoTDStartGame/SM_DoupoTDStartGame` is now drilled as the third focused doupotd packet pair. The client takes `DoupoTDData:GetFightPartnerVoList()`, iterates it with `Kpairs`, and pushes every value into `CM_DoupoTDStartGame.indexList` via `indexList:Add(v)` before sending. The server response also carries an `indexList`; the handler calls `DoupoTDMgr:DoupoTDStartGame(msg)`, which guards scene/start state, sets `V_StartGame=true`, and raises `DoupoTDType.EventType.OnStartGame`. This pair is a start-confirm boundary: selected fight partners are visible in the request, while actual local start state is gated by the server response.
94. `CM_DoupoTDReadyFright/SM_DoupoTDReadyFright` is now drilled as the fourth focused doupotd packet pair. The request packet declares `levelId`, but current visible `CM_DoupoTDReadyFrightFun` does not fill it before sending; keep this as a schema/static-fill gap, not proof that the field is impossible to carry through pooled/default state. The server response carries `gameState/levelId/grade/skillList/gameType/currRefreshWave/wave/exp/gameHp`; the handler calls `DoupoTDMgr:DoupoTDReadyFright`, then `DoupoTDModel/DoupoTDData` write `V_GameLevelId`, `V_CurWave`, `V_PlayerGrade`, `V_PlayerExp` when not already in game, `V_GameType`, `WallMaxHp`, `V_CouncilSkillList`, rebuild fight-partner cache, and raise `IniDataFinish` plus `SceneInfoSync`. This is the main scene-ready snapshot boundary.
95. `CM_DoupoTDUnlock/SM_DoupoTDUnlock` is now drilled as the fifth focused doupotd packet pair. The request packet declares `Id`, but current visible `CM_DoupoTDUnlockFun` does not fill it before sending, so it remains a schema/static-fill gap. The server response carries `partnerList`; on `msg.code==0`, the client calls `DoupoTDData:UpdateDDPartnerVos(msg.partnerList)`, which iterates partners into `UpdateOneDoupoTDCharacterVo -> DoupoTDCharacterVo:SetServerData`, storing `serverMsg`, level, and final attrs. It also records newly unlocked ids for `OpenDoupoTDNewCharacterView(msg.partnerList,true)` and raises `Update_Character_Info`. This is an unlock-result boundary: server-owned partner data updates local character state and UI.
96. `CM_DoupoTDChange/SM_DoupoTDChange` is now drilled as the sixth focused doupotd packet pair. The request packet declares `changeHp`, and the response packet declares `gameHp`, but `CM_DoupoTDChangeFun` only obtains a pooled message and sends it, while `SM_DoupoTDChangeFun` only emits a debug log. Searching the full exported `by_source/lscripts` tree finds no visible call site for `CM_DoupoTDChangeFun` and no extra `changeHp` assignment beyond the generated packet read/write class. Treat this as a registered-but-thin placeholder/light sync packet. The actual wall HP initialization observed so far is still the `ReadyFright`/scene-init path that writes `msg.gameHp` or `msg.gameInitHp` into `DoupoTDData:SetWallMaxHp`.
97. `CM_DoupoTDChangeInit/SM_DoupoTDChangeInit` is now drilled as the seventh focused doupotd packet pair. It is not a wall-HP path: both packets only declare `id:Int`. The server handler gates on `msg.code==0`, calls `DoupoTDData:SetGameInitChar(msg.id)`, raises `RedDotID.DoupoTDInfoFirstBattle`, and raises `RefreshGameInitChar`. Static search shows `DoupoTDData:GetGameInitChar` returns `self.gameInitCharId or 1`, but no visible doupotd Lua definition for `SetGameInitChar`; the parallel `towerdefense` module defines the setter and request fill explicitly. Also, doupotd opens `Window.DoupoTDFirstBattleSelectView`, but that view source is not visible in exported lscript coverage. Treat this as a first-battle/initial-character selection boundary with an unresolved visible-source gap around the selector view or setter/request fill, not as combat authority logic.

Do not spend more static time on this first-battle selector unless adding runtime observation; move to another doupotd pair such as `CM_DoupoTDSelect/SM_DoupoTDSelect`.
101. `CM_DoupoTDSelect/SM_DoupoTDSelect` is now drilled as the eighth focused doupotd packet pair. It is the upgrade-skill candidate generation/reroll boundary. The request is small and explicit: `type` and `grade`, filled from `DoupoTDUpgradeSkillView` using `DoupoTDType.GetSkillType.UpLevel=1` or `Random=3`. The response returns `selectVOS`, `canRandomValue`, `type`, and `grade`; `selectVOS` resolves to `DoupoTDSelectVO(index,type,id)`. On success, the client expects three candidate VOs, writes remaining random count into `V_RandomSkillNum`, sorts and stores candidates in `V_RandomSkillSkillIds`, then raises `AnewRandomSkill` for the UI. This pair does not apply the selected upgrade; selection click flows into `CM_DoupoTDEffect/SM_DoupoTDEffect`.
102. `CM_DoupoTDEffect/SM_DoupoTDEffect` is now drilled as the ninth focused doupotd packet pair. It is the application boundary after `Select`: the request sends one `index` chosen from the candidate list, and the response returns `effectVO:DoupoTDEffectVO(index,type,id)`. `SM_DoupoTDEffectFun` updates local skill/effect state and raises UI/scene refresh events. Current static interpretation of `effectVO.type`: `1` is treated as a new role/character acquisition for the `DoupoTDUpgradeSkill` event payload; `2` maps to `DoupoTowerDefense_SkillEnhance`, increments `V_RoleSkillAttrList`, `V_RoleSkillIdList`, `V_RoleSkillEnhanceEffList`, and updates the selected list; `3` maps to `DoupoTowerDefense_TurnSkillEnhance` and adds its `rogueId` effects into `V_RoleSkillEnhanceEffList`.
103. `CM_DoupoTDPan/SM_DoupoTDPan` is now drilled as the tenth focused doupotd packet pair. The visible trigger is combat-side: when `DoupoTDEntityMgr` handles a killed wave monster and `cfg.objectType` is elite, it calls `CM_DoupoTDPanFun()` with no grade, so the request sends `grade=0` unless another caller supplies a value. The server returns `effectVOList`, which the client converts to a simple id list for `DoupoTDDialInfoView`, while also broadcasting the full VO list through `RefreshUltraSkill`. This is a server-driven elite-kill reward/ultra-skill dial branch. Scene rendering keeps only `DoupoTowerDefense_TurnSkillEnhance` rows whose config type equals `DoupoTDType.PanSkillType.skill=1`; the dial UI itself is a 16-slot presentation (`DialCount=16`).
104. `CM_DoupoTDCanSkill/SM_DoupoTDCanSkill` is now drilled as the eleventh focused doupotd packet pair. It is a partner-specific strengthenable-skill query/cache boundary. The request sends `id=partnerId or 0`; the response returns the same `id` and `skillList`, then stores that list under `V_CanStrengthenRoleAttrInfoList[id]` and raises `RefreshCanStrengthenRoleSkill`. Static search in the visible doupotd module shows no caller for `CM_DoupoTDCanSkillFun` and no doupotd strengthen-select UI files, while the parallel `towerdefense` module has `TowerDefenseStrengthenSelectSkillView` consumers. Treat doupotd request-side UI as an export/source gap; the response cache shape is confirmed.
105. `CM_DoupoTDPreInfo/SM_DoupoTDPreInfo` is now drilled as the twelfth focused doupotd packet pair, and it is intentionally marked as not fully closed. The packet schema declares a strengthening prefetch snapshot (`holdVOList`, `partnerVOS`, `skillPoint`, `preWare`), but visible doupotd `SM_DoupoTDPreInfoFun` reads `gameInitHp` and `partnerVO` even though those fields are not in `SM_DoupoTDPreInfo.lua`; only `skillPoint/preWare` align with schema. No visible doupotd caller sends `CM_DoupoTDPreInfoFun`. The parallel `towerdefense` module is the control path: it calls `CM_TowerDefensePreInfoFun` during activity activation and handles the same schema by populating strengthen holes, partner strengthen attrs, skip-wave, and total points. For doupotd, treat this as a registered packet with a schema/handler version mismatch and visible caller gap. The pair-flow generator now emits `*_pair_msg_fields.tsv` so these mismatches are machine-visible.
106. `CM_DoupoTDSaveHold/SM_DoupoTDSaveHold` is now drilled as the thirteenth focused doupotd packet pair. It is the first clean strengthening-hole update pair in this branch: the request sends `index` and `partnerId`, and the response returns `holdVOList/partnerVOS`. `SM_DoupoTDSaveHoldFun` writes the hole map through `SetStrengthenRoleHoleInfo`, writes each returned partner VO through `SetStrengthenRoleAttrInfoList`, then raises `RefreshStrengthenRoleHole` and `RefreshStrengthenRoleSkill`. The new `pair_msg_fields.tsv` shows no packet-specific schema mismatch here. The remaining gap is request-side UI/caller: visible doupotd Lua has no caller for `CM_DoupoTDSaveHoldFun`, while `towerdefense` has `TowerDefenseStrengthenSelectRoleItem` as the corresponding role-slot picker.
107. `CM_DoupoTDSaveSkill/SM_DoupoTDSaveSkill` is now drilled as the fourteenth focused doupotd packet pair. It is the adjacent partner-skill strengthening save path: both request and response carry `partnerVO:DoupoTDFightPartnerVO`; the response writes that VO to `V_StrengthenRoleAttrInfoList[partnerVO.id]` and raises `RefreshStrengthenRoleSkill`. Schema/handler alignment is clean. The request-side gap matches `CanSkill/SaveHold`: visible doupotd Lua has the Data-layer selection helpers (`CheckSelectStrengthenAttr`, `CheckUnSelectStrengthenAttr`, temp skill list) but no visible strengthen-select UI or caller. `towerdefense` provides the expected control path: `TowerDefenseStrengthenSelectSkillView` builds a temporary partner VO from chosen skillList and calls `CM_TowerDefenseSaveSkillFun(partnerVO)`.
108. `CM_DoupoTDUpLevel/SM_DoupoTDUpLevel` is now drilled as the fifteenth focused doupotd packet pair. The request sends `id`, but no visible doupotd caller has been found. The response schema is narrower than towerdefense: doupotd `SM_DoupoTDUpLevel` only declares `partnerList`, while the visible handler also reads `gameInitHp` and `partnerVO`; `pair_msg_fields.tsv` marks those as missing from packet-specific schema. The meaningful state write is `UpdateDDPartnerVos(partnerList)`, which updates each `DoupoTDCharacterVo` via `SetServerData`, including level and final attrs. Treat `SetWallMaxHp(msg.gameInitHp)` and `Update_Character_Info(msg.partnerVO,true)` as stale or version-mismatched handler reads unless runtime evidence proves otherwise. TowerDefense is the control path where the up-level response genuinely includes `gameInitHp/skillPoint/preWare` and visible panel/player views call the request.
109. `CM_DoupoTDReset/SM_DoupoTDReset` is now drilled as the sixteenth focused doupotd packet pair. This one is schema-clean: the request sends `id`, the response returns `partnerVO:DoupoTDPartnerVO`, and the handler consumes only `ClientResult.code` plus that schema field. On success it calls `UpdateOneTowerPartnerVo(partnerVO)`, raises `Update_Character_Info`, and refreshes red dots through `UpdateTowerRedDot()`. Static doupotd still has no visible caller for `CM_DoupoTDResetFun`; ordinary `towerdefense` provides the control UI path from `TowerDefensePlayerView` to `CM_TowerDefenseResetFun(self.defenseVo.id)`.
110. `CM_DoupoTDInfo/SM_DoupoTDInfo` is now drilled as the broad doupotd activity snapshot entry. It is triggered by `DoupoTDMgr.UpdateActivationActivityVO` and `CheckActivityActivation` when doupotd activity/PVE state opens or changes. The response schema has only `passList`, `doupoTDPartnerVOS`, and `doupoTDPartnerNoActiveVOS`; downstream `SeDoupoTDInfoFun(msg)` consumes all three cleanly. `passList` is transformed into `V_NewLevelIdDic[type][layer]` for level completion checks, active partner VOs update `serverMsg/lv/finalAttrs`, and non-active partner VOs update only `finalAttrs`. The pair-flow generator now records downstream target/related `msg.xxx` consumers in `*_pair_msg_fields.tsv`, which is important for handlers that forward the whole response object to model/data code.
111. `CM_DouPoCardPanel/SM_DouPoCardPanel` is now drilled as the draw/card panel snapshot. It sits next to `Info` in `DoupoTDMgr.UpdateActivationActivityVO`: when the `DouPoTdDraw` activity is open, the client requests this panel data after `CM_DoupoTDInfoFun()`. The response has `pageValue`, `cardVOList`, `taskRewardList`, `allCardNum`, and `currRewardId`; the handler caches the whole response as `V_LastestDrawInfo`, raises the draw-reward red dot, and broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardPanel(msg)`. The composition panel consumes `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`, and the draw panel consumes `currRewardId`; `cardVOList` elements are `DouPoCardVO(resourceId)`, while `taskRewardList` is written as an int list.
112. `CM_DouPoCardDraw/SM_DouPoCardDraw` is now drilled as the actual card draw action. The request sends `id` and `times`; `DoupoTDDrawPanel.OnClickDrawBtn` selects the draw-config id and caps `times` at `10` based on available draw item count. The response returns `douPoCardVOS`, `rewardResults`, `allCardNum`, and `currRewardId`; NetLogic only raises the draw-reward red dot and broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardDrawEvent(msg)`. `DoupoTDDrawPanel.OnDrawEvent` consumes the event, plays the draw animation, turns each `DouPoCardVO.resourceId` into a display item via `DoupoCardCompose_ComposeCard.showItem`, appends `rewardResults`, and opens the reward popup. Static Lua does not show this handler updating `V_LastestDrawInfo`, so `Panel/TaskReward` remain the visible cache-update paths for `currRewardId`.
113. `CM_DouPoCardTaskReward/SM_DouPoCardTaskReward` is now drilled as the draw-progress reward claim. The request sends the current progress reward id from `DoupoTDDrawPanel.currentTaskRewardId`. The response returns `id`, `rewardResults`, `alreadyNum`, and `currRewardId`; the handler opens the reward popup with `rewardResults`, writes `currRewardId` through `SetCurrentTaskReward`, raises the draw-reward red dot, and broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardTaskReward(msg)`. This is the visible path that updates `V_LastestDrawInfo.currRewardId` after claiming progress rewards; `alreadyNum` is in the schema but not consumed by the visible handler slice.
114. `CM_DouPoCardCompose/SM_DouPoCardCompose` is now drilled as the single-card composition action. The request sends the current page plus two card slot indexes. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel` only sends after validating same character, same quality, same resource id, different slots, and not max star. The response returns `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`; NetLogic broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardCompositing(msg)`, and the composition panel refreshes the card list, updates the current page, and plays a normal or special effect based on the source card quality.
115. `CM_DouPoCardComposeAll/SM_DouPoCardComposeAll` is now drilled as the batch composition action. The request has no fields and is only sent from `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnBatchBtnClick` when the panel is not tweening and `GetCanCompositingCardReDot()` is true. The response mirrors single-compose page refresh shape: `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`; NetLogic broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardCompositingAll(msg)`, and the panel plays batch effects before refreshing the card list and clearing tween state.
116. `CM_DouPoCardMerge/SM_DouPoCardMerge` is now drilled as the card-page sorting/collating action. The request has no fields and is sent by `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnCollatingBtnClick`. The response returns only `cardVOList/pageValue`; NetLogic broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardPageSort(msg)`, and the panel refreshes the card list after forcing visible items. This response intentionally lacks `allCardNum`, unlike the panel/compose/compose-all page refresh responses.
117. `CM_DouPoCardJumpPage/SM_DouPoCardJumpPage` is now drilled as the card-page navigation action. The request sends `jumpPageValue`, sourced from the composition panel previous/next callbacks after guarding invalid or same-page indexes. The response returns `pageValue/cardVOList/allCardNum`; NetLogic broadcasts `DouPoPartnerCardPageChange(msg)`, and the composition panel reuses `OnCommonRefreshEvent` to refresh the visible card page. This closes the remaining visible card-page navigation pair.
118. `CM_DouPoCardRedCan/SM_DouPoCardRedCan` is now drilled as the batch-composition availability flag. The request packet exists and has no fields, but current visible Lua only registers it; no `CM_DouPoCardRedCanFun` or sender was found. The response returns `can:Bool`; NetLogic writes `DoupoTDModel.V_CanCompositingCardReDot=msg.can` and raises the composition red dot. `DoupoTDCompositingPanel.OnBatchBtnClick` uses `GetCanCompositingCardReDot()` as the gate before sending `CM_DouPoCardComposeAll`, so this is the state source for whether the batch compose button is actionable.
119. `CM_DouPoCardTopQ/SM_DouPoCardTopQ` is now drilled as the card-mode max-quality/max-star cache refresh. The request has no fields and is sent from visible partner/up-battle scene entry points when `DifferentType.card` is active. The response maps partner/card ids to max quality and max star through `id2q/id2Star`; `DoupoTDData` stores those maps, `DoupoTDModel` raises `DouPoPartnerCardMaxQualityEvent`, and UI items use them to render partner backgrounds and star labels. This is display/state-cache data, not a mutation action.
120. `CM_DoupoTDUpgrade/SM_DoupoTDUpgrade` is now drilled as the in-run player-grade upgrade boundary. The request sends local accumulated `addExp`; `DoupoTDEntityMgr.ExpChange` optimistically updates local `PlayerGrade/PlayerExp` and queues pending `sendServerExp` before sending, while reconnect can resend pending exp. The response returns authoritative `exp/oldGrade/grade`; the handler subtracts confirmed exp from `sendServerExp` and schedules `DoupoTDUpgradeSkillView`, which leads into the already-covered `Select -> Effect` skill upgrade selection/application chain.
121. `CM_DoupoTDSkipLevel` is now documented as a single-sided skip intent. The request packet has no fields and is sent from `DoupoTDInfoPanel` only after `allowSkipLevel` and total-level checks plus user confirmation. There is no visible dedicated `SM_DoupoTDSkipLevel`; the result path uses `SM_DoupoTDGamePlayer.isSkipLevel`, which drives reward/new-character display, resets skip state, and refreshes the info panel. Do not model it as a missing pair unless a future export reveals a real SM packet.
122. `SM_DoupoTDActivityEnd`, `SM_DoupoTDGMBattle`, and `SM_DoupoTDGMClubBattle` are now classified as server-only surfaces. `ActivityEnd` is an activity-end push that requests finish-game handling. The two `GM*Battle` handlers cache an immortal/club doupo finish VO and immediately request replay by `replayId`; their names and lack of visible CM request mark them as debug/admin/server-driven replay entry points rather than normal player mutation flows.
123. `lua_lscript_module_doupotd_packet_coverage_inventory_report.md` now confirms the visible doupotd NetLogic registration surface is fully classified: `58` registered packets, `28` CM, `30` SM, `54` pair-report covered, `1` single-flow covered, `3` server-only covered, and `0` unclassified. This does not mean all gameplay semantics are fully understood, but it does mean the packet registration map has no unknown CM/SM entries left in current exports.

Important network/domain facts:

- Bootstrap/config: `https://prod-config-frxxz.akbing.com/config/android`
- CDN/resource roots include `https://cdn-frxxz.akbing.com/...`, `https://cdn-frxxz2.akbing.com/...`, `https://clientapp-frxxz.akbing.com/...`
- Login/server-list endpoint: `https://prod-login-frxxz.akbing.com/game/server`
- Server check endpoint: `https://prod-login-frxxz.akbing.com/game/check_server`
- Manifest has `usesCleartextTraffic=true`; network security config trusts `user/system` certs. This matters for local proxy/capture, but socket payloads still need separate parsing.

## Proven Login Chain

JADX now confirms the Java SDK shell in readable source, including the embedded SQwan plugin APK layer. The custom DEX scanner remains useful as a dependency-free structural check, while `apk_jadx_login_source_report.md` and `apk_jadx_sq_plugin_core_report.md` are the easier human-facing source reports.

Embedded plugin layer:

```text
PluginManager.loadPlugin
  -> copyAssetPlugin(defaultPluginApkName, "plugin")
  -> ApkClassLoader(plugin.apk)
  -> PluginSQwanCore reflects com.sqwan.msdk.SQwanCoreImpl
  -> SQwanCoreImpl.getPlatform
  -> new _SQwan(...)
  -> BaseSQwanCore.login / doLogin
  -> _SQwan.loginPlatform / Platform.loginSQ
  -> com.sy37sdk.core.SQwan.login
  -> Platform.sqLoginSuccess
  -> Bundle token/gid/pid
```

37 account module layer:

```text
com.sy37sdk.core.SQwan.login
  -> SQwanManager.login
  -> IAccountMod.login
  -> AccountModImpl.login
  -> UIVersionManager.getLoginController
  -> FastVerifyController / AccountLoginManager
  -> AccountLogic.accountLogin or phone/fast/wechat login helpers
  -> signed SqRequest HTTP endpoints
  -> AccountLogic.handleLoginSuccess
 -> Map(userid, username, token, pid, gid)
  -> SQwanManager.convertMapToBundle
```

sy37 login response surface:

```text
AccountLogic.createUserInfo(responseString)
  -> response JSON fields: uid / uname / token / refresh_token / login_account / action_type / pwd
  -> sy37 UserInfo(uid, uname, token, refreshToken, alias, actionType, loginType, loginWay)
  -> AccountCache.setUserInfo / AccountTools.setAccountToFile / UserInfoManager.setLoginUser
  -> success map: userid / username / token / pwd / pid / gid / account_type / login_type
  -> SQwanManager.convertMapToBundle
  -> Platform.sqLoginSuccess final Bundle(token, gid, pid, userid, username)
  -> FlameUnityActivity.SDKLogin serializes "1__token__gid__pid"
  -> SDKCallback.OnSDKLoginData
  -> UnityPlayer.UnitySendMessage(GameEnter, OnReceiveLogin, data)
```

Use `apk_jadx_sy37_login_response_surface_report.md` when asking how SDK HTTP login responses become Unity login input. This report records static field names and callback boundaries only; it does not read account cache, capture live responses, or store real token/account values.

Login token to socket handoff:

```text
FlameUnityActivity.SDKLogin(Bundle token/gid/pid)
  -> SDKCallback.OnSDKLoginData("1__token__gid__pid")
  -> UnityPlayer.UnitySendMessage(GameEnter, OnReceiveLogin, data)
  -> PhoneMsgReceiver.OnReceiveLoginData(data)
  -> AccountInfo.V_LoginToken / V_GameId / V_PId
  -> CsCallLuaMgr.GetLoginTokenSucceed(LoginToken, GameId, Pid, Uid, TimeStamp)
  -> LoginMgr:GetSDKServerInfo(Pid, Token)
  -> GetServerInfo:GetSDKServerList(Pid, LoginToken)
  -> server-list response builds LoginServer / curServerItem
  -> EnterGameInfo:ContinueLogin()
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_LoginFun(curServerItem.serverId, curServerItem.account, curServerItem.pid, V_ChannelPackage)
  -> CM_Login.writing()
  -> SocketManager:F_SendMsg(CM_Login)
```

Use `login_token_to_socket_handoff_report.md` for the joined Java/IL2CPP/Lua evidence map. Current true run confirms `48` evidence rows and `9` field-source rows. Important nuance: the observed Java shell login path emits only `status/token/gid/pid`, while IL2CPP has optional `Uid/TimeStamp` slots and still forwards five args to Lua. `CM_Login.sign/signTime` are not the raw Java payload string; Lua fills them from `LoginModel:GetLoginAccountData().V_Token / V_Time` before packet write. This report remains static-only: no live tokens, signatures, replay scripts, or APK changes.

Login account sign source:

```text
LoginMgr:LoginCheck(LoginToken, GameId, ChannelId, Uid, TimeStamp)
  -> data = {token, uid, bundleId, bundleVersion, cid, device, devId, gid, time}
  -> GameLoginBridge.F_WebLoginCheck(callbackId, LoginToken, LuaUtil.encode(data))
  -> GameInitSettingModel.F_GetSettingValue(ImportServerUrl=12 or TestLoginCheckInUrl=13)
  -> FileUtil.F_LoadFilePost(login-check-url, jsonData, success, error)
  -> CallBackManager.CallStringDelegate(callbackId, responseText)
  -> LoginMgr:StartInfo(jsonData)
  -> StartGameInfo:LoginAccountDataInfo(jsonData)
  -> LuaUtil.decode(jsonData, typeof(LoginAccount))
  -> LoginAccount.FillData: V_Token=data.token, V_Time=data.time
  -> LoginModel:SetLoginAccountData(loginAccount)
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_LoginFun reads V_Token/V_Time as sign/signTime
```

Use `login_account_sign_source_report.md` when asking where `CM_Login.sign/signTime` originate. Current true run confirms `39` evidence rows, `6` field-source rows, and `4` URL config rows. The production `setting.config` has `ImportServerUrl` and `TestLoginCheckInUrl` pointing at `https://prod-login-frxxz.akbing.com/sdk/mobi37/login/verify`; the embedded older/default asset config also keeps `frxxz-test1.eyugame.com` variants. The important boundary is that `CM_Login.sign` is the game login-check response `LoginAccount.V_Token`, not a locally generated MD5/signature in the Lua `CM_LoginFun` path.

Login socket response flow:

```text
SM_Login(20002)
  -> LoginNetLogic.SM_LoginData(msg)
  -> LoginMgr:LoginDataBack(msg)
  -> EnterGameInfo:StartEnter_4(msg)
       token/channel/time/cross/openServer setup
  -> EnterGameInfo:StartEnter_5(msg)
       mapInfo/role/worldLevel/wallet/functionOpen model setup
       RoleMgr.InitRole(msg.role) or create-role branch
       SceneMgr.SceneNetLogic.CM_RecoverMapFun(false)
  -> GameTime.UpdateSystemTime marks V_IsInfoTime
  -> EnterGameInfo.CheckAndSendFinish()
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_LoginFinishSent()
  -> CM_LoginFinish(20007)
```

Use `lua_login_socket_response_flow_report.md` when asking what the login server reply initializes. Current true run confirms `61` evidence rows, `16` `SM_Login` fields, and `16` field-consumer rows. Key field consumers are `token -> curServerItem.token`, `timestamp/timeZone -> GameTime`, `crossVO -> UpdateCrossData`, `openServer -> self.openServer`, `mapInfo -> V_EnterGameMapInfo`, `role -> RoleMgr.InitRole/create-role branch`, `wallet -> WalletData.SetWalletInfo`, and `functionOpen -> FunctionMgr.Model.DataInfo`. This report remains static-only: no live socket payloads, no token values, no replay client.

Login finish post-sync fanout:

```text
EnterGameInfo.CheckAndSendFinish()
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_LoginFinishSent()
  -> LoginModel.RaiseEvent(LoginSucceed)
  -> EnterGameInfo.LoginFinishGetServerData()
       -> EventMgr.NetLogic.CM_EventSyncAllFun()
       -> MedicineMgr.GetClientAttributes()
       -> SubpackageMgr.StartInit()
       -> XianLvMinesMgr.NetLogic.CM_SyncBattleFieldBuffsFun()
       -> WorldlevelMgr.NetLogic.CM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSyncFun()
       -> if Travel open: YoulipoolMgr.NetLogic.CM_YouliPoolInfoFun()
       -> PhoneHelper.F_UploadThinkingLaunchProcess(GameLogin_Success)
```

Use `lua_login_finish_post_sync_report.md` when asking what the client requests immediately after login succeeds. Current true run confirms `22` evidence rows, `8` post-login entries, and `7` response fields. Closed/mapped packet pairs include `CM_EventSyncAll(38005) -> SM_EventSyncAll(38006)`, `CM_SyncBattleFieldBuffs(87641) -> SM_SyncBattleFieldBuffs(87642)`, `CM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSync(10083) -> SM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSync(10084)`, and conditional `CM_YouliPoolInfo(14371) -> SM_YouliPoolInfo(14372)`. `MedicineMgr.GetClientAttributes()` remains a manager-call entry. `SubpackageMgr.StartInit()` is manager-level in this first fanout report, but the follow-up handler probe now closes it to `CM_SubpackageSync(43203) -> SM_SubpackageSync(43204)`.

Login post-sync handler drill-down:

```text
LoginFinishGetServerData()
  -> WorldlevelMgr.NetLogic.CM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSyncFun()
       -> CM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSync(10083)
       <- SM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSync(10084)
       -> WorldlevelData.SetWorshipInfo(msg)
       -> WORLD_LEVEL_WORSHIP_INFO_SYNC
       -> WorldLevelWorshipView.UpdateView(msg)

LoginFinishGetServerData()
  -> SubpackageMgr.StartInit()
       -> SubpackageModel.CheckLoadingAndStart()
       -> SubpackageMgr.GetServerData()
       -> CM_SubpackageSync(43203)
       <- SM_SubpackageSync(43204)
       -> SubpackageModel.V_SubPackageRecRewardList = msg.subpackageIds
```

Use `lua_login_post_sync_handlers_report.md` when asking what the first post-login sync packets do locally. Current true run confirms `44` evidence rows, `5` handler rows, `8` response fields, `5` closed Lua handlers, and `0` schema-only handlers. The closed handlers are event sync, XianLvMines battlefield buffs, world-level worship info, YouliPool info, and subpackage reward/download sync. The earlier schema-only conclusion was an export/index coverage artifact.

`lua_login_post_sync_protocol_families_report.md` is the compact index for those same branches. Current true run confirms `5` families and `43` packet/VO rows: `player.event` has `16` rows, the battlefield-buff slice of `player.partner` has `CM_SyncBattleFieldBuffs / SM_SyncBattleFieldBuffs / BattleFieldBuffVO`, `world.worldlevel` has `10` rows, `player.inner.youlipool` has `10` rows, and `player.subpackage` has `4` rows. All `5` post-login response packets now have visible handler names in the rebuilt canonical catalog. Use this before opening generated packet files manually.

`lua_login_post_sync_unresolved_handler_gaps_report.md` is now a gap-resolution report for the three formerly open branches. Current true run confirms `event_sync_all`, `xianlv_mines_battlefield_buffs`, and `travel_youlipool_info` all have visible fanout calls plus response schemas and are classified as `resolved_visible_handler`. The candidate surface count rose because the full raw lscript export exposes the concrete NetLogic functions.

`lua_login_post_sync_manager_source_gaps_report.md` now confirms the manager-source issue is resolved after raw lscript export. Current true run confirms `LuaInitializer` has the expected `require(...)` and `AddSingleton(...)` paths, and exact `EventMgr.lua`, `XianLvMinesMgr.lua`, and `YoulipoolMgr.lua` source/package hits are present in `by_source/lscripts`.

`lua_login_post_sync_cpp2il_manager_surface_report.md` checks the obvious native-readable companion surface. Current true run finds no exact Cpp2IL hit for the required Lua manager package names or the target send/response handler functions. The only positive `EventMgr` hits are generic C# event bridge/infrastructure, so this does not close `EventSyncAll`. The remaining likely routes are hidden/generated Lua source, XLua/Lua loader metadata, or carefully scoped runtime observation.

`lua_login_post_sync_lua_loader_boundary_report.md` connects that gap back to the asset index. Current true run scans the `hot_update_lscripts_text_assets.tsv` inventory (`13078` rows) and confirms no exact manager assets or required package strings for the three missing managers, while Cpp2IL still shows the generic Lua loading path. This narrows the static path: if these managers are present, they are outside the current exported text-asset inventory or are generated/loaded in a way the current extractor did not materialize.

`lua_login_post_sync_raw_lscript_bundle_gaps_report.md` then checks the raw downloaded resource tree directly. Current true run confirms the target raw bundles exist under `frxx_game_files\lscripts\gamesystem\game`, but their bundle hashes are missing from the current text-asset index and `by_source/lscripts` export. The strongest new evidence is `event_4202f391f2fa425c1cd3864ebd848b62.bytes`, which contains an `EventMgr.lua` marker in raw bytes. Treat this as an extractor/index coverage gap: next work should make raw lscript bundles exportable, then rerun the manager/handler probes against the expanded Lua surface.

`lua_login_post_sync_raw_lscript_handler_closure_report.md` performs that targeted export for the three missing bundles and closes the previously schema-only post-login branches. Current true run exports `7 + 48 + 13 = 68` TextAssets and confirms `EventMgr.lua/EventNetLogic.lua/EventData.lua`, `XianLvMinesMgr.lua/XianLvMinesNetLogic.lua/XianLvMinesModel.lua`, and `YoulipoolMgr.lua/YoulipoolNetLogic.lua/YoulipoolModel.lua` are present. The closed sinks are `EventData.EventInfo(events)`, `XianLvMinesModel.SetBattleFieldBuffs(msg.activityBaseIdToBuff)`, and `YoulipoolModel.SetInfoMap(infoMap)`.

`lua_raw_lscript_export_coverage_report.md` generalizes the lesson from those three bundles. It compares every raw `frxx_game_files\lscripts/**/*.bytes` bundle against the current `by_source/lscripts` export tree by bundle hash/stem. The initial true run was `505` raw bundles, `163` covered, `342` missing. After `lua_raw_lscript_missing_export_report.md`, refreshed coverage is `505` raw bundles, `505` covered, and `0` missing. This report remains the first stop before claiming a Lua module/source is absent; the old `hot_update_lscripts_text_assets.tsv` is still a hot-update diff inventory, not the full raw lscript surface.

`lua_lscript_surface_inventory_report.md` is the current full exported Lua surface map. It writes bundle/module/asset TSVs and labels each asset origin as `hot_update_diff`, `raw_missing_export`, or `preexisting_or_manual_export`. Use `lua_lscript_surface_modules.tsv` to choose the next module-level reverse target; raw-missing-only modules such as `doupotd`, `digitdoor`, `theoutcast`, `dungeon`, `club`, and `hiddenworld` are newly useful because their game logic was not covered by the earlier hot-update diff inventory.

`lua_lscript_module_doupotd_surface_report.md` is the first focused module surface map built from the full raw-lscript export. It writes files/functions/requires/markers/protocol-ref TSVs for one module. The `doupotd` true run shows the useful drill-down pattern for future modules: start from `*_surface_files.tsv` to find the largest model/manager/NetLogic files, then use `*_surface_protocol_refs.tsv` and `*_surface_markers.tsv` to choose the next protocol or state-flow probe.

`lua_lscript_module_doupotd_netlogic_flow_report.md` slices `DoupoTDNetLogic.lua` function bodies into request fields and local sink edges. Use `*_netlogic_flow_fields.tsv` for client request payload fields, and `*_netlogic_flow_edges.tsv` for `F_SendMsg`, `GetMessageFromPools`, `Model:*`, `Mgr.Inst_get()`, and `RaiseEvent` lines. This is static Lua evidence only, but it is now a compact entry point for the doupotd gameplay protocol.

`lua_lscript_module_doupotd_protocol_schema_report.md` joins that NetLogic flow to `parsed_configs/lua_packet_index`. Use `*_protocol_schemas.tsv` for packet id/direction/schema status and `*_protocol_fields.tsv` for ordered packet fields. Current true run has full schema coverage for the NetLogic packet set and confirms every directly assigned request field matches the packet schema.

This is the compact schema map for decoding or explaining the login reply without opening each generated Lua VO file by hand.

`lua_sm_login_nested_vo_depth2_report.md` expands the typed second-level beans under that snapshot. Current true run confirms `3` objects, `6` fields, and `3` parent-child relations: `RoleVO.hangPoint -> HangPointVO(40076).values`, `RoleVO.skill -> SM_SkillVO(30065).posList/learnList`, and `MapInfoVO.position -> Grid3DVO(40052).x/y/z`. Generated Lua does not expose element types for the remaining list/map fields, so the report keeps those as list/map fields rather than guessing.

sy37 request envelope layer:

```text
SqRequest.of(url).signV3()
  -> RequestBuilder tag SignVersion.V3
  -> addParamsTransformer(CommonParamsV2 or CommonParamsV3)
  -> login-specific fields such as uname/upwd/mobile/ticket/auth_code plus D-Token/trans_info where applicable
  -> SqHttpClient.createBuilder
  -> RequestIdOkInterceptor(Request-Id, Request-LiveId)
  -> SignInterceptor dispatches SignVersion.V3
  -> SignV3Interceptor adds sign from sorted params + appKey tail + lowercase MD5
  -> GateWayEncryptInterceptor may encrypt whitelisted secure query/body payloads
```

Use `apk_jadx_sy37_request_signing_report.md` for the static evidence. Treat it as a request-envelope map, not a request-forging recipe.

sy37 URL update layer:

```text
BaseSQwanCore.init
  -> UpdateUrlManager.reqUrlUpdateManager
  -> SqRequest.of(UrlConstant.UPDATE_URL).signV3().CommonParamsV1().GET
  -> response api_infos[] with api_key/api_info
  -> ordinary entries: apiMap + apiUrls
  -> x_secure_key entry: GateWayManager.updateKeySet
  -> GateWayManager.updateWhiteList for -secure URLs
  -> UrlUpdateUtils.setUrlData + SharedPreferences sq_url_update
  -> UrlUpdateUtils.urlUpdate reflects @UrlUpdate static fields
  -> ProtectUrlManager/SqHttpDns protect-host refresh
```

Use `apk_jadx_sy37_url_update_report.md` when comparing hardcoded SDK URLs with runtime/network observations.

sy37 updateable URL catalog:

```text
apk_jadx_sy37_url_catalog.tsv
  -> one row per @UrlUpdate static String field
  -> class_name / field / url_key / x_key / inline_expr / assignment_expr / effective_expr / url_family

apk_jadx_sy37_url_catalog_by_class.tsv

```

Use the catalog as the static map of possible SDK HTTP/WebSocket endpoints, and the URL update report as the runtime rewrite mechanism.

sy37 endpoint usage catalog:

```text
apk_jadx_sy37_endpoint_usage.tsv
  -> one row per SqRequest.of(...) callsite

apk_jadx_sy37_endpoint_usage_by_endpoint.tsv

  -> joins the @UrlUpdate catalog when the call uses a static URL field
```

Current real scan: `75` `SqRequest.of(...)` callsites, `63` joined to the static `@UrlUpdate` catalog, `44` secure-like callsites, `51` POST and `13` GET. Main signature shape remains `V3` (`58` callsites); common parameter transformers are mostly `CommonParamsV3` (`33`) and `CommonParamsV2` (`15`). There are `12` non-catalog expressions, now classified by `usage_category`: WebView proxy GET, live-show static fields, M/S-layer generic helpers, bootstrap URL update, device-info upload, custom-domain probe, and stored login-popup URL fallback.

Use `apk_jadx_sy37_endpoint_usage_report.md` when asking "which code path actually calls this SDK endpoint, with which request envelope shape?". It is a static callsite map only: it does not send requests, execute signing code, or collect runtime token values.

sy37 wrapper endpoint catalog:

```text
apk_jadx_sy37_wrapper_endpoint.tsv
  -> one row per higher-level wrapper call such as post(INewUrl.LOGIN, ...)

apk_jadx_sy37_wrapper_endpoint_by_helper.tsv

```

Current real scan: `24` wrapper endpoint rows, all joined to the static `@UrlUpdate` catalog. `21` rows are `s_layer_legacy_account_post_helper` (`INewUrl.*`, `SCommonParam`, `V2`), and `3` rows are `m_layer_generic_post_helper` (`IMUrl.*`, `CommonParamsV1`, `V2`). This complements endpoint usage: `SqRequest.of(str)` shows the sink/helper, while wrapper endpoint rows recover the upstream concrete URL fields.

## Proven Server/Socket Chain

Formal server-list path:

```text
GetSDKServerList
  -> GameLoginBridge.F_GetServerList(callbackId, jsonData, isZip)
  -> prod-login-frxxz.akbing.com/game/server
  -> LoginData/LoginServer
  -> IntoGame(sd.V_Host, sd.V_Port)
  -> LoginMgr:SetServerData(domain, port, ...)
  -> SocketManager:F_InitSocketCon(curServerItem.domain, curServerItem.port, ...)
  -> SocketBridge.F_Connect
```

Cpp2IL now confirms the HTTP bridge details for the server-list leg:

```text
GameLoginBridge.F_GetServerList(callbackId, jsonData, isZip)
  -> GameInitSettingModel.F_GetSettingValue(ServerListUrl = 8)
  -> String.Format(settingUrl, jsonData, isZip)
  -> FileUtil.F_LoadFilePost(url, jsonData, successDelegate, errorDelegate)
  -> CoroutineManager.StartCoroutine(...)
  -> success: gzip bytes + UtilCompress.DecompressFromGzip + UTF8, or DownloadHandler.get_text
  -> CallBackManager.CallStringDelegate(callbackId, responseText)
```

The production config evidence remains `https://prod-login-frxxz.akbing.com/game/server`; the APK also carries older/default `frxxz-test1.eyugame.com` URL config entries.

Cpp2IL also confirms the shared `FileUtil.F_LoadFilePost` loader shape:

```text
F_LoadFilePost(url, postData, finishFunc, error)
  -> Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes(postData)
  -> new UnityWebRequest(url, method)
  -> UploadHandlerRaw(bytes)
  -> CertificateHandler()
  -> SetRequestHeader(header, value)
  -> DownloadHandlerBuffer()
  -> SendWebRequest()
  -> success: finishFunc.Invoke(downloadHandler)
  -> error/timeout: error.Invoke(request.error), Dispose(), optional retry
```

The exact request-header name/value are not resolved by the current ISIL report. Keep that as a small evidence gap unless a later string-usage mapper ties the metadata string literal to the `SetRequestHeader` operands.

Lua now confirms the response-side handoff into the socket target:

```text
CallBackManager.CallStringDelegate(callbackId, responseText)
  -> GetServerInfo callback(jsonData)
  -> LuaEventMgr.RaiseEvent(GET_SERVER_LIST_SUCCEED, jsonData)
  -> ServerGroupListView.F_ServerListUpdateFun(jsonStr)
  -> LoginModel.SetServerListData(jsonStr)
  -> LuaUtil.decode(jsonStr, typeof(LoginData))
  -> LoginData.FillData(data).ServerInfo(data.servers)
  -> LoginData.ServerVoInfo: LoginServer.FillData(server)
  -> LoginServer.V_Host = data.host / V_Port = data.port
  -> WinLogin or AutoIntoGame passes V_Host/V_Port into LoginMgr.IntoGame
  -> EnterGameInfo.StartEnter_1 -> SetServerData + SocketConnect
  -> SocketBridge.F_Connect(pIp, pPort, ...)
```

The effective server-list schema is `{code, data}` optional wrapper, with `data.servers[]` carrying `host`, `port`, `id`, `server`, `name`, `group`, and related display/state fields. The local fallback emits the same `data.servers` shape.

Login socket packet schema already observed:

- `CM_Login = 20001`
- `SM_Login = 20002`
- `CM_ReLogin = 20009`
- `SM_ReLogin = 20010`
- `CM_ProtoHash = 20013`
- `SM_ProtoHash = 20014`

`CM_Login` fields include `account/serverId/pid/cid/gid/device/devId/pushToken/bundleId/bundleVersion/location/channelPackage/signTime/sign`.

Lua send-side packet flow now confirmed:

```text
SocketConnected / EnterGameInfo.StartEnter_2
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_ProtoHashFun()
  -> CM_ProtoHash(20013) via SocketManager.F_SendMsg
  -> SM_ProtoHash(20014): hash/version
  -> LoginMgr.ContinueLogin(msg)
  -> EnterGameInfo.StartEnter_3(msg) checks hash/version
  -> EnterGameInfo.ContinueLogin()
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_LoginFun(curServerItem...)
  -> CM_Login(20001).writing()
  -> BaseMessage.write -> LusuoStreamWarp -> ProtoBridge
  -> SocketManager.DoSendMsg -> LuaSocket.F_Send(pid, sn)
  -> SocketBridge.F_Send(pid, isMainSocket, sn)
```

`CM_Login` wire order is: `account:String`, `serverId:Int`, `pid:String`, `cid:String`, `gid:String`, `device:String`, `devId:String`, `pushToken:String`, `bundleId:String`, `bundleVersion:String`, `location:String`, `channelPackage:Int`, `signTime:Int`, `sign:String`. `CM_ReLogin(20009)` is the relogin branch with `account/pid/serverId/token`.

Lua receive-side login response flow now confirmed:

```text
MessagePool handler for SM_Login(20002)
  -> LoginNetLogic.SM_LoginData(msg)
  -> error branch when msg is nil or msg.code != 0
  -> LoginMgr.LoginDataBack(msg)
  -> EnterGameInfo.StartEnter_4(msg)
       V_SM_Login = msg
       curServerItem.token = msg.token
       ChangeChannelPackage(msg.channelPackage)
       GameTime.TimeDataInfo(msg.timestamp, msg.timeZone)
       SdkCustomEvent.SmLoginInfo(msg)
       UpdateCrossData(msg.crossVO)
       openServer = msg.openServer
       StartEnter_5(msg)
  -> StartEnter_5(msg)
       V_EnterGameMapInfo = msg.mapInfo
       Task/WorldLevel/Wallet/FunctionOpen/Role model init
       RoleMgr.InitRole(msg.role) or create-role branch
       SceneMgr.SceneNetLogic.CM_RecoverMapFun(false)
  -> GameTime.UpdateSystemTime marks V_IsInfoTime
  -> EnterGameInfo.CheckAndSendFinish()
  -> LoginNetLogic.CM_LoginFinishSent()
  -> CM_LoginFinish(20007)
```

`SM_Login` local field order is: `accountId:String`, `token:String`, `role:RoleVO`, `mapInfo:MapInfoVO`, `wallet:MessageList2List`, `inner:Bool`, `timestamp:Long`, `timeZone:Int`, `worldLevel:Int`, `functionOpen:FunctionOpenVo`, `totalOnlineSecends:Long`, `totalTimes:Int`, `openServer:Long`, `crossVO:CrossVO`, `channelPackage:Int`, `clubVO:SimpleClubVO`, followed by its `ClientResult` super read. The field-consumer table in `lua_login_socket_response_fields.tsv` is the best current compact view of how this login snapshot feeds local models.

`lua_sm_login_nested_vo_report.md` expands the first-level login snapshot objects. Current true run indexes `6` objects and `65` fields: `RoleVO(30051)`, `MapInfoVO(40058)`, `FunctionOpenVo(30710)`, `CrossVO(59603)`, `SimpleClubVO(80192)`, plus `SM_Login` itself. Use its `lua_sm_login_nested_vo_fields.tsv` before manually opening individual VO files.

`lua_sm_login_nested_vo_depth2_report.md` expands the typed bean refs one level deeper: `HangPointVO(40076)` is a `values` map, `Grid3DVO(40052)` is `x/y/z` floats, and `SM_SkillVO(30065)` is `posList/learnList`. Other first-level list/map fields remain unresolved element-type surfaces in generated Lua.

After the login finish gate, `LoginFinishGetServerData()` starts the first visible post-login sync fanout:

- `CM_EventSyncAll(38005) -> SM_EventSyncAll(38006)` for event list sync.
- `CM_SyncBattleFieldBuffs(87641) -> SM_SyncBattleFieldBuffs(87642)` for partner/battlefield buff map sync.
- `CM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSync(10083) -> SM_WorldLevelWorshipInfoSync(10084)` for world-level worship state; this request send is fully visible in `WorldlevelNetLogic`.
- `CM_YouliPoolInfo(14371) -> SM_YouliPoolInfo(14372)` for travel/Youli pool info, guarded by `FunctionType.Travel`.
- `MedicineMgr.GetClientAttributes()` and `SubpackageMgr.StartInit()` are visible manager calls but not pinned to concrete Lua packet sends by the current export.

Cpp2IL now confirms the native send-frame handoff:

```text
ProtoBridge.WriteInt / WriteBigString
  -> shared LusuoStreamQuick write buffer
  -> SocketBridge.F_Send(proId, isMainSocket, sn)
  -> SocketManager.F_Send(proId, isMainSocket, sn)
  -> ByteSocket.F_Send(proId, sn)
  -> header stream: WriteInt(sn), WriteInt(proId)
  -> packet stream: WriteNoCompress(body_length + 12), header stream, body stream
  -> Socket.BeginSend(packet_buffer, 0, packet_length, ...)
```

`WriteBigString` is length-prefixed UTF-8 bytes. `WriteInt` is controlled by `LusuoStreamQuick.isCompress`; the compressed branch has varint/zig-zag style operations, which is serialization compression, not proof of cryptographic encryption. Treat the `body_length + 12` frame shape as a strong static hypothesis for send-frame construction; receive-side parsing now confirms the length-head/body/dispatch architecture, but exact runtime byte widths still need a packet sample if we want a final wire spec.

Cpp2IL now confirms the native receive-frame and dispatch handoff:

```text
Socket.Receive(4-byte length head)
  -> ByteUtil.ReadInt(head)
  -> Socket.Receive(body bytes)
  -> ByteSocket.ReadPackage()
  -> LusuoStreamQuick(buffer, length, littleEndian=false, isMsgCompress)
  -> ReadInt(...) packet header fields
  -> PoolMessageManage.IsLuaMessage(proId)
  -> PoolMessageManage.read(proId, stream)
  -> CSMessagePool.F_GetMessage(proId)
  -> packet.reading(...)
  -> CSMessagePool.F_SendHandler(proId, message)
  -> Lua MessagePool handler / LoginNetLogic callback
```

Concrete login receive examples are pinned: `SM_Login=20002`, `SM_ProtoHash=20014`, and `LoginNetLogic` registers both handlers. The exact semantic names/order of the `ReadPackage` header integers are still not final; avoid over-naming them until a tighter probe or live capture confirms the fields.

parsed_configs/lua_packet_index/packet_registrations.tsv   # MessagePool.F_Register sites and handlers

Feature subset counts: `login=38`, `bluestarsea=30`, `blld=20`, `gongfa=109`, `fight=133`, `faze=42`. Examples now line up cleanly: `CM_Login(20001)` has 14 write fields, `SM_Login(20002)` dispatches to `SM_LoginData`, `SM_ProtoHash(20014)` dispatches to `SM_ProtoHashFun`, and BlueStarSea packets such as `CM_BlueStarSeaPurify(98004)` / `SM_BlueStarSeaPurify(98005)` show both wire fields and `BlueStarSeaNetLogic` handlers.

The static chain is now explicit: `CM_GongFaUpgradeTimes.upgradeList` is only a client request list; `SM_GongFaUpgradeTimes.upgradeList[]` is the source used by the client to update `GongFaItemVO`, aggregate `rewardResults` into local `exp`, merge `attrs.addAttrs`, take the last `attrs.finalAttrs`, and call `GameUtil.DealAttrChangeByModule(allAttrs, exp)`.

The chat-share href click path now has a separate TextEx/Lua callback report:

```text
apk_static_index/hot_update_gongfa_homemake_share_href_flow.tsv
apk_static_index/hot_update_gongfa_homemake_share_href_edges.tsv
apk_static_index/hot_update_gongfa_homemake_share_href_report.md
```

Current true run: `320` runtime/Cpp2IL evidence rows and `10` semantic edges. Main conclusion: C# `TextEx.OnPointerClick` only hit-tests `HrefInfo` boxes and dispatches raw `HrefInfo.m_Content` through `CallBackManager.CallStringDelegate`; it does not understand `66`. Lua `LuaTextEx:AddHyperClickEvent` registers the string callback through `LuaCallBackMgr` and stores its id via `TextExBridge.AddHyperClickListener`. The business consumer is `LuaGlobal.OnHyperLink -> HyperLinkMgr.DealWithHyperLinkStr -> HyperLinkType.LingJieSkill -> DealWithLingJieSkill`, where `66|createTime` becomes `data[1]`, then `ChatModel:GetChatShareLingJieSkillInfo(createTime)` opens `GongfahomemakeMgr:OpenCreateSkillDetailView(vo,true)`. Remaining gap: visible `ChatLabelChatCell.lua` sets `labelContent` text and long-press behavior, but does not show `labelContent:AddHyperClickEvent(LuaGlobal.OnHyperLink)`; likely prefab/generated/runtime binding or another non-exported initialization path.

API for rerun:

```text
POST /api/fanxiu/resources/hot-update/gongfa-homemake-share-href-probe
```
